| [2180](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/2180) | dawn | src/tests/unittests/validation/RenderPipelineValidationTests.cpp | 126 | 2018-11-03 05:10:50.000000000 | I'd like to discuss a interesting issue. The array of descriptor.modules will have a default object for descripotr.modules[1]. What's more, the dawn shader stage enum use 0 to represent vertex. So this cheating success!(Indeed I did a lot of validations but in this case it can passed without any problem). So do you have any ideas ?  One of the proposal is to use dawn::ShaderStageBit when ShaderModule get their execution Model.So the default 0 means shader stage none and validation will find this.  And I'm curious about why dawn has both dawn::ShaderStage and dawn::ShaderStageBit? it is a little duplicated. |

| [6340](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/6340) | dawn | src/tests/end2end/VertexFormatTests.cpp | 281 | 2019-04-26 01:37:22.000000000 | Yes, the reason why it should be in shader is that for x86 architecture, float operation will be extended to 80-bit. So I moved all of the algorithm into shader and that's work. And I'll add comment to explain this. |

| [8320](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/8320) | dawn | src/dawn_native/vulkan/BindGroupVk.cpp | 93 | 2019-06-26 01:39:56.000000000 | The error presents when running compute shader dynamic offset test case:  Descriptor set 0x2415 bound as set #0 encountered the following validation error at vkCmdDispatch() time: Dynamic descriptor in binding #0 index 0 uses buffer 0x240d with dynamic offset 256 combined with offset 0 and range 264 that oversteps the buffer size of 264.  And by reading vulkan spec, in VkDescriptorBufferInfo , it says, "range is the size in bytes that is used for this descriptor update, or VK_WHOLE_SIZE to use the range from offset to the end of the buffer".  Currently, the binding range are always set to binding.size which maybe a whole buffer size.So this will trigger range + offset > buffer.size.   Set this value with VK_WHOLE_SIZE or the real size used in shaders can fix this issue. |

But, first, this d3d12SerializeRootSignature API has been superceded by D3D12SerializeVersionedRootSignature as of the Windows 10 Anniversary Update (14393). I'd like to replace this one but decide not to add review workload in this patch.  Second, assume we choose 64DWORD. For me, an ASSERT for root signature means we need to compute the size by ourselves through rootSignatureDescriptor in dawn(maybe not?), I agree this will help to not call into driver with large root signature, but I think maybe check this in driver and catch the error by ASSERT_SUCCESS is ok?  Third, should dawn only use 64DWORD? Even the second-half doesn't commit a performance improvement. |

| [30960](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/30960) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 244 | 2020-10-27 10:19:35.000000000 | (Copy from origin CL, left by Corentin:) We're working in 2D so if we need to support rotations, 2 by 2 is enough, if we need to support translations then 3 by 3 is enough. But if my concern is not correct, I'm happy to compare the float value (in a - b < threshold way). WDYT? |

| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2021-03-10 01:19:23.000000000 | The basic idea for this test case is that I need this tests to make sure I could rely on the samplers(not sure I call it correctly, which I mean is the directly assign from source to dst) to handle value transformation magically. So I need to emulate what's the transform looks like based on specs and compared it with the result.  I think the complicated part is due to the FP16 part and src->dst transformation in cases like 8-channel format -> 10+2 channel format. And if we accept uint, sint or srgb format as inputs and output, it will have more helper functions and conditions in this test.  But I'm happy to do some simplification based on your further comments. |

| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2021-03-08 08:42:06.000000000 | Double check the failure, it is a mesa driver bug in 19.0.2 but has been fixed in latest mesa(ubuntu 20.04 default driver, verified on local) The case hangs on when running without suffix _e_use_tint_generator(I'm not quite sure the difference). I'm not quite sure whether we should workaround it(since it has been fixed by new mesa). What's your suggestions? And PTAL for the CL, thanks! |
| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2021-03-10 01:19:23.000000000 | (Oops, I think I should reply to the failure reason here too). I've dumped the spirv-asm and do some experiment on different mesa version. I added the analysis result as comments in CL(Could do a bisect for mesa to find the exactly CL fix this issue if you think necessary). The result is: NOTE: There is a bug in Mesa before version 19.1.0 which cannot handle variables as index to access vector values correctly. It hangs the execution of shader. Tint generator uses 'clamp' to prevent OOB access for index access and it could workaround this bug too. But for the test cases which doesn't use tint generator, it won't generate OOB guard ops and will hit this bug on bots(which running with Mesa 19.0.2) and causes bots crash. To workaround this crash, we use {vector}.r/g/b/a instead of 'loop' + {vector}[i] to access channels in the test shader.  So I think tint generator has no need to workaround it. WDYT? |
| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2021-03-15 09:06:36.000000000 | FYI: Also file an issue about using variable as index to access vector with Spirv-cross will hang on linux with mesa driver < 19.1.0 here https://bugs.chromium.org/p/tint/issues/detail?id=638  |
| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2021-03-08 08:33:36.000000000 | Double check the failure, it is a mesa driver bug in 19.0.2 but has been fixed in latest mesa(ubuntu 20.04 default driver) The case fails when running without suffix  |

| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | src/tests/end2end/CopyTextureForBrowserTests.cpp | 209 | 2021-03-18 08:45:53.000000000 | Ah, I got your point. Thanks for more explain and agree it is redundant to TextureFormat and remove the extra swizzle test. Done. |
| [44020](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/44020) | dawn | src/tests/end2end/CopyTextureForBrowserTests.cpp | 209 | 2021-03-18 01:36:02.000000000 | (Maybe I'm too careful on checking this case. ( Because I've done the wrong+wrong = the test case passed before)). Take copy from RGBA8Unorm to BGRA8Unorm case as example, in shader we check them like: success = success && aboutEqual(srcColor.r, dstColor.r) && aboutEqual(srcColor.g, dstColor.g) && aboutEqual(srcColor.b, dstColor.b) && aboutEqual(srcColor.a, dstColor.a). This cannot catch one case: the driver do both wrong(and the case can pass). Like, when calling \\|CopyTextureForBrowser\\|, the driver write value (0.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0)// rgba from RGBA8Unorm to BGRA8Unorm texture but not doing swizzle. So the dst texture is (0.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0) // bgra. And when sampling from BGRA8unrom texture, the driver made mistake again by not swizzling the order at all. In shader, we could still got (0.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0), and the test pass. So adding a read-back to CPU buffer and check the value is the guard for this(to make me feel confidence when the case tell me it is "PASS"). |

| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | dawn.json | 850 | 2021-11-25 02:18:44.000000000 | Good question. TBH, chromium has the ability to support some customer color space if they could provide transform method about tranferring to XYZD50 color space. I'm not 100% understand them for now but per my current understanding from this doc https://skia.org/docs/user/color and chromium related codes. 1. Chromium defined color space with 4 IDs(They all have some predefined enums), PrimaryID, TransferID, MatrixID, RangeID.   - 1.a according to the list, it covers most common cases(I think), including HDR          cases      2. Chromium support customer color space if you could provide toXYZD50 transform matrix and transferFn. 3. The transformation has two steps, transfer to XYZD50 from source color space and then transfer from XYZD50 to dst color space. This provide ability to support almost all of the color space conversions().    3.a the steps could be simplified in some situations 4. ICC Profile I referred https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/skia/include/third_party/skcms/skcms.h;l=156;drc=de68be3f18ba99cc01d75903e167ca09bade253c. But not quite sure whether this could handle all cases. |

| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | dawn.json | 880 | 2021-12-08 04:11:43.000000000 | (More thoughts) So, finally, we need to get something like skcms_ICCProfile(https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/skia/include/third_party/skcms/skcms.h;l=156;drc=94c6fda0960a28d2f7083624c6643ae89cb8d57f) in blink side for input ExternalImage. And for dst color space, we could get similar infos too. So we need to do precompute in blink side for : 1. For dst color space, get the inverse matrix of toXYZD50. 2. Precompute the conversion matrix by dst.fromXYZD50 * src.toXYZD50. 3. Compute the encoding transferFn from the known decoding transferFn  > dstTransferFunction: enum { linear, srgb } // doesn't make sense to specify the whole color space because it would make it non-orthogonal to the conversion matrix Why this doesn't transfer all parameters but with these enums? I assume that this needs Dawn to keep some parameters.  And we could transfer final results to CopyTextureForBrowser with: - needColorSpaceConversion: bool // fast path to skip conversions. - srcAlphaMode: enum { premul, unpremul } - srcTransferFunctionParameters: f32 A,B,C,E,F,G,epsilon - 3x3 f32 conversion matrix - dstTransferFunctionParameters: f32 A,B,C,E,F,G,epsilon - dstAlphaMode: enum { premul, unpremul }  WDYT? |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | dawn.json | 880 | 2021-12-08 00:45:46.000000000 | > we would move all of the ColorSpaceTable data to Blink where it's probably already known? No, chromium doesn't have it. Chromium calculate all of the matrices and transferFn parameters in runtime(Chromium only have the basic color space metas, e.g. rx, ry, gx, gy, bx, by and white point). So I think we still need to have the similar table in blink.  The only concern here is the data it transfers between client and server. With ID, we have less bytes to transfer and the serialization/deserialization doesn't touch the value we need in conversion. BTW, Chromium has a comments in the ColorSpace IDs part but I'm not quite sure I fully understand it. It says,  "// Used to represet a color space for the purpose of color conversion. // This is designed to be safe and compact enough to send over IPC // between any processes. " (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/gfx/color_space.h;l=59) |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | dawn.json | 880 | 2021-12-08 02:36:40.000000000 | > The main thing I don't know is whether or how Chromium would get from a user-provided image file to one of these color spaces. Maybe it would always use the "CUSTOM" modes.  I think they have a ICCProfile compare function. https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/skia/third_party/skcms/skcms.cc;l=1567;drc=94c6fda0960a28d2f7083624c6643ae89cb8d57f and do things like this https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/skia/src/core/SkColorSpace.cpp;l=196;drc=94c6fda0960a28d2f7083624c6643ae89cb8d57f;bpv=0;bpt=1 |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | dawn.json | 880 | 2021-12-08 03:31:19.000000000 | (Take CopyExternalImageToTexture() implementation in blink side in consideration) The only place we could get the color space info from ExternalImage is PaintImage generated from StaticBitmapImage. It has methods to return pointer to SkColorSpace object. But SkColorSpace doesn't have any methods that could return transferFn params or conversion matrix. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/skia/include/core/SkColorSpace.h;drc=94c6fda0960a28d2f7083624c6643ae89cb8d57f;l=104) It provides a function to compare whether to SkColorSpace are the same.  If we don't add methods in SkColorSpace to return those meta infos, the logic should be: in gpu_queue.cc or a new gpu_xxx.h file, we hold the conversion matrix and transferFn for known supported color space. Create SkColorSpace with them and compare it with the input. Then transfer the matrix and transferFn params to Dawn.  But we don't have abilities to support CUSTOM color space, I think. So maybe we should expand the SkColorSpace API? |

| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 64 | 2021-11-25 08:55:09.000000000 | I think this 1.13711894 is intentional by chromium. | [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 64 | 2021-11-30 09:17:29.000000000 | Done |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 69 | 2021-12-07 09:29:20.000000000 | Done |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 70 | 2021-12-08 08:42:38.000000000 | Done |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 72 | 2021-12-08 08:42:38.000000000 | Done |
| [70780](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/70780) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 72 | 2021-12-08 00:59:20.000000000 | Oh, I see. There is some difference between chromium implementation and css spec here. It seems that in css color space spec, we need to handle sign always(https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#color-conversion-code). But in chromium code to generate decoding/encoding result, it has a extended_ attributes to decide whether we needs sign() and abs()(https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/gfx/color_transform.cc;l=328;drc=94c6fda0960a28d2f7083624c6643ae89cb8d57f;bpv=1;bpt=1).  And for display-P3, it generates the code that doesn't consider the sign but for extended-srgb, it uses it.  I think we could align with css color space spec here. |

| [81482](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/81482) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2022-02-24 05:54:05.000000000 | > If in Chromium, the planes are separate SharedImages, then maybe it's better having a 1:1 SharedImage -> WGPUTexture association instead of a 1:1 IOSurface -> WGPUTexture mapping. Or - is there a plan for a single SharedImage with multiple planes?  (My understanding, ccameron@ pls correct me if I'm wrong) Unfortunately, on current chromium side, the shared image will wrap the whole CVPixelBuffer but expose the single plane infos. So the videoFrame contains two SIs and each of them wrap the same CVPixelBufferRef but expose different plane info. But I'm not sure whether there is plan to have a single video shared image (It would be ideal if there is such plan).  > Also, for RG16Unorm and R16Unorm, please feel free to add them as a Dawn extension.  Ack. |

Then Creating per plane mtlTexture from IOSurface when user creating texture view on this texture through Dawn and WGPUTextureView holds the per plane mtlTexture.  Do you think this is the correct direction in Dawn? If yes, I'll iterate on this CL to make it mature. |

| [81482](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/81482) | dawn | src/dawn/native/metal/TextureMTL.mm | 826 | 2022-03-18 02:30:32.000000000 | @cwallez, @enga, @ccameron Let me describe the cases I'm considering and see which is the possible solution. The VideoFrame which we could access in blink might hold: - Multiple SIs (and multiple mailboxes) but the single IOSurface - Single SI and single IOSurfcae (But stands for multiplanar data, not the current implementation states yet) - Multiple SIs and multiple IOSurface (Each IOSurface stores a single plane).  So in blink, we probably do the things like: ``` TextureView plane_texture_views[kMaxPlane]; // Check VideoFrame Plane number for (uint32_t plane = 0; plane < plane_number; ++plane) {   gpu::webgpu::ReservedTexture reservation = webgpu->ReserveTexture(device_);   webgpu->AssociateMailbox(videoFrame->mailboxes[plane], usage, reservation.texture ...);   plane_texture_views[plane] = reservation.texture->CreateView(&viewDesc); }  WGPUExternalTextureDescriptor external_texture_desc = {}; external_texture_desc.plane0 = plane_texture_views[0]; external_texture_desc.plane1 = plane_texture_views[1]; external_texture_desc.colorSpace = WGPUPredefinedColorSpace_Srgb;  // Create GPUExternalTexture. ```  So: - I think `AssociateMailbox` with plane ID might not be a good idea. Because we might have the case creating texture view with wrong plane index with the generated gpuTexture(e.g. gpuTexture is generated with AssociateMailbox(plane0), but createTextureView has aspect Plane1). This means we needs to add some validation which is not necessary at all.  - Current CL requires creating TextureView explicitly before any ops. AssociateMailbox is lightweight (Creating WGPUTexture and adding Ref). But It is weired to support the usage like copy.  - The proposal above which will do creating all MTLTextures in InitializeFromIOSurface phase will fix the issue by creating MTLTextures from all planes and holds them. But I think it might have duplicated works when handling the case that VideoFrame has multiple SIs but the single IOSurface. In the second AssociateMailbox, we'll creating a new WGPUTexture and creating real MTLTextures again. I'm not sure whether we could avoid this duplicated work. If there is a way, I think this probably the best solution.  Do you have any suggestions? |

So I think macOS could have similar cache mechanism to store IOSurface and created plane MTLTextures. I'll have a try on this. |
| [81482](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/81482) | dawn | src/dawn/native/metal/TextureMTL.mm | 826 | 2022-03-21 05:41:21.000000000 | Re-re my answer: On mac there is a bit hard to have similar cache. One big problem is where should we store these pre-built MTLTextures. SIs, WGPUTextures and Dawn Device are not good places. The only way to store it is we should have a shared_state object which is similar to D3D backend. I suspect it is worth on mac. On Windows D3D, the shared_state could store shared_handle which re-open is expensive. But on mac, I think CFRetain/CFRelease is light-weight. So my question above is still available.   cwallez@, enga@ and ccameron@, WDYT? |

If they're equal, the device state is marked as Idle(And without any pending AsyncTask ). But the mCompltedSerial, mLastSubmittedSerial and mFutureSerial are updated in the Tick() call. For example, check supports will consume more time when we pass an array of ExternalSemaphoreHandles and we have (assume we have) an array of service implementation, which is a loop-in-loop check. | [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2023-03-16 00:42:18.000000000 | >Could we instead use the naturalSize for video element here?  Yes and that's the follow up blink side changes. We should set source.width and source.height there to natural size. That helps the source origin validation in blink side. But when we call CreateExternalTexture()(https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/modules/webgpu/gpu_queue.cc;l=614;drc=0e9a0b6e9bb6ec59521977eec805f5d0bca833e0;bpv=0;bpt=1), we don't use that source.width and source.height. Instead, we choose visible rect from media::VideoFrame in CreateExternalTexture() for both WebCodec::VideoFrame and HTMLVideoElement.  Technically, we could pass natural size to CreateExternalTexture() and create ExternalTexture with that natural size but I hesitate to do it because: - If we add extra attribute in WGPUExternalTextureDescriptor, pass natural size to ExternalTexture and doesn't generate internal shader with natural size, it is a bit trivial. - If using natural size instead of visible size to create ExternalTexture and generate internal shader, it means we might do two scale/crops. First is from frame coded_size to visible rect(That's what current ExternalTexture do, crop/downscale or no changes), second is from visible_rect to natural size(possible to upscale and downscale). Current internal shader for ExternalTexture doesn't support upscale, we need to add it. - I'm hesitate to add the upscale ability for internal shader of ExternalTexture because it is a special thing for CopyEI2T(and copyEI2T has its own extra scale step to handle it https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp;drc=08027c662e35c50d1a08bd59c5ea405b0d337108;l=412). For importExternalTexture() generated GPUExternalTexture, crop video frame to visible rect is enough (it provides the exactly content from origin video frame without extra scales). If user want a natural size result, they could issue a rendering pipeline to do the up-scale/down-scale themselves. |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2023-03-21 06:03:34.000000000 | According to comments in https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/pull/3960, I think we'll use natural size as source size for CopyEI2T. Address some comments, pls take a look again, thanks! |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2023-03-15 07:44:11.000000000 | The reason is that for ExternalTexture (created from WGPUExternalTextureDescriptor), visible rect is the correct size. We sample/load texels from the ExternalTexture instead of copy from it. Visible rect describes origin video frame.  But for CopyEI2T, it is a bit different. CopyEI2T means a copy and it is important for user to know what is the source size(I proposed a PR in spec about this https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/pull/3960). User in js cannot get visible rect from HTMLVideoElement. Instead, they can get intrinsic size (which is natural size in chromium implementation) from HTMLVideoElement.videoWidth/videoHeight.  So this CL is based on the fact that ExternalTexture requires video frame visible rect but copyEI2T requires video frame natural size. But yes, we could also do a simple change that adding WGPUExternalTextureDescriptor with the natural size for copyEI2T to use it. |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2023-03-09 08:01:34.000000000 | PTAL, thanks! |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2023-03-17 01:17:27.000000000 | BTW, still wait for feedback about spec PR https://github.com/gpuweb/gpuweb/pull/3960 to see whether this CL behaviour is correct. |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | src/dawn/native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 701 | 2023-03-17 01:19:12.000000000 | Ah, got it. That maybe Ok because copyTextureForBrowser is used internally in blink (We can control the behaviour). |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | src/dawn/native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 701 | 2023-03-16 00:47:27.000000000 | >GPUExtent2D defaults to (1, 1) so defaulting would require people to explicitly fill out the zeroes, which isn't super helpful.  That surprise me. Does dawn.json side default value not works(https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/dawn.json;drc=69e1c4b638d3909635ceb26b6b652a6a2cc28fe7;l=1376)? |
| [123380](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/123380) | dawn | src/dawn/native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 704 | 2023-03-17 01:15:26.000000000 | > Do we have a test that if the naturalSize is larger than the source size things work correctly? That's the good point, I think I need to add it.  >what would even be the correct behavior? Right now we discard? But should we put black instead? No, we don't discard. That's the interesting part for why we pass natural size here. In copyTextureForBrowser, we upload scale params here (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp;drc=08027c662e35c50d1a08bd59c5ea405b0d337108;l=413). But that sacle is based on natural size and copy size. For example, assume we have natural size larger than video frame size, and copy size equal to natural size, we could still generate correct texcoord in current shader (which I think 0.0, 0,0 for the top-left and 1.0, 1.0 for the bottom-right) and use that texcoord to sample ExternalTexture maps to the video frame origin size.   So I think we're doing things like, calculating a scale factor with natural size and copy size but apply the scale factor result in ExternalTexture visible rect by sampling with generated coord. |

| [125900](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/125900) | dawn | src/dawn/native/vulkan/external_memory/MemoryService.cpp | 121 | 2023-03-31 02:52:46.000000000 | Austin Eng [a] Right now, all the nullptr checks should never happen, because usages are guarded by SupportsImportMemory and SupportsCreateImage. | [125900](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/125900) | dawn | src/dawn/native/vulkan/external_memory/MemoryService.cpp | 129 | 2023-03-31 02:52:46.000000000 | Austin Eng if the image type isn't supported, then there will be no service found.  Instead, return nullptr, and then in IsSupported implementations, check service != nullptr && service->SupportsImportMemory(...) ?  Shaobo Yan Addressed return nullptr. But not quite sure what is IsSupported used for? Adding nullptr checks in Service API implementations. And I think SemaphoreService side also need this change (with follow-up CL).  Austin Eng IsSupported checks are so we can check it just once before doing GetMemoryImportParams and ImportMemory, so we shouldn't need the other checks at [a] they could be ASSERTs.  Given that we always should check support first, it makes me think that slightly restructuring the API could be beneficial. So that you need to check support before even getting the object on which to call GetMemoryImportParams and ImportMemory. That said, this is complex enough right now that I think we should keep the structure the same for now and revisit it after we can support runtime selection of the service. There's other things that should eventually be improved as well - see https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/vulkan/DeviceVk.cpp;l=928;drc=8101d6d81854e4f437e0f03f77694a01aaa8df7a;bpv=1;bpt=1  (https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/125482/comment/f95be66b_7bc7c4bb/) |

| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ExternalTexture.cpp | 338 | 2024-04-10 08:19:40.000000000 | >It seems loadTransformMatrix = Scale(coordTransformMatrix, mVisibleSize)?  Ok, I think I'm also wrong about this. For example, a video frame [240, 480] size, with rotation 180. After applying rotation, it got the matrix: - for norm coord, mat2x3\\|-1, 0, 1, 0, -1, 1 \\|                    - for non norm coord , mat2x3 \\|-1, 0, 240 0, -1, 480\\|                         These two matrix doesn't have linear transformation. But a simple rules here is that replace the last columns of norm matrix by multiplied frame size will get the non norm coord matrix. |
| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ExternalTexture.cpp | 338 | 2024-04-10 02:07:22.000000000 | >It seems loadTransformMatrix = Scale(coordTransformMatrix, mVisibleSize)?  A little bit different. Developers provide exact coord(which should be in visible rect) for `textureLoad()` so we don't need to do the scale compute that coordTransformMatrix has.  (And I'm a bit worry about float precision during computation. But that seems not that important? not sure.)   So maybe we could do things like: - Pass a transformMatrix in ExternalTextureParams but only handles rotation/mirror - Pass visible size and visible origin in ExternalTextureParams - Pass frame size in ExternalTextureParams.  So we could use shader to do the final cropping steps instead of pre-baked it.  If we do this, - For TextureSampleClampToEdge()   - multiply transform matrix   - do scale based on framesize and visible size   - do translate based on visible origin and frame size.  - For textureLoad(), we need to do   - multiply transform matrix   - do translate with visible origin.    WDYT? |

| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/multiplanar_external_texture.cc | 411 | 2024-04-26 03:11:02.000000000 | It reminds me that current OOB test is too strict. It requires, (-1, -1) to clamped to bottomLeft always and (LargerThanWidth , LargerThanHeight) clamp to topRight always. But according to spec, it requires(https://www.w3.org/TR/WGSL/#textureload): - The data for some texel within bounds of the texture  - A vector (0,0,0,0) or (0,0,0,1) of the appropriate type for non-depth textures  - 0.0 for depth textures |

| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/tint/lang/wgsl/ast/transform/multiplanar_external_texture_test.cc | 387 | 2024-04-22 06:55:23.000000000 | Done |
| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/tint/lang/wgsl/ast/transform/multiplanar_external_texture_test.cc | 561 | 2024-04-16 06:23:26.000000000 | >I still don't understand why the loadMatrix cannot be computed easily from the sampleMatrix.  Maybe how we handled rotation caused this. We handled rotation by using -1 * coord and x, y switching. So the last column of the matrix is the offset to make negative coords to positive. And I don't think we have simple way to change the last column of sampleMatrix only by computing. |

| [197556](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/197556) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2024-07-10 08:59:52.000000000 | Not sure whether this plan works to resolve https://issues.chromium.org/issues/42240537  The idea is that PipelineLayout doesn't hold native layout anymore. Native Pipeline instead holding native layout. |
| [197556](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/197556) | dawn | src/dawn/native/Pipeline.h | 115 | 2024-07-11 00:28:46.000000000 | They're different immediateData. For pipelineLayout, it stores user provided immeidate data range. For Pipeline, it stores dawn internal used immediateData size, or current called push constant range.  There is another design: PipelineLayout host all internal + external push constant range, but defer initialization to pipeline creation. And it holds native pipelineLayout. In this design GPUPipelineLayout might have 1-N mapping with native pipelineLayout due to dawn internal push constant (Decided by shader side.). |
| [197556](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/197556) | dawn | src/dawn/native/Pipeline.h | 115 | 2024-07-11 00:57:24.000000000 | >Patchset 2 8:54 AM there's not enough code for me to have a clear picture of what you plan to do, but the thing that immediately I'm confused about is what immediate data size you use to make the backend pipeline layout.  I think that's the point. I'll upload the follow-up draft soon.  >If it is, how is the layout to backend layout 1:1 since there could be multiple pipelines which share the same layout?  Yes, it is. And I plan to make backend pipeline holds the backend pipelineLayout. And GPUPipelineLayout doesn't hold backend pipeline layout anymore. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2024-09-11 02:34:33.000000000 | Thanks for the reading and comments!  >Querying stuff after transforms is actually something that the Tint team wants to remove over time, it just hasn't done it for HLSL yet.  That's a big change. Still possible to achieve "use exact numbers of immediate data slots". Tint Reflection might provide enough infos (as you mentioned in below comments about useVertexIndex). But I'm not sure whether some transforms e.g. "ArrayLengthFromUniform" (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/tint/lang/wgsl/ast/transform/array_length_from_uniform.cc;drc=a6941c6ee100b201f6f02c12e90fa8c05c3a607f;l=261) (which only emit (from 0 to) highest dynamic storage buffer(analysis in trasforms) size variables) might generate builtins or additional codes which uses part of immediate datas.  To be clear about the concern, take "vertex index offset" as an example, tint reflection told us no vertexIndex or instance index has been used, but after applying some hlsl specific transforms, some generated code uses them. So we got false info from tint reflection.   But it might be Ok. API side could know the final result by query proper tint reflection info combined with API side toggle/workaround/extra command utils info.  But still need your wise about this.     >Let me know if you'd like a small meeting to discuss all of this, I know it's a lot but a clear solution is in sight!  Thanks! That would be great! | [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 164 | 2024-09-10 08:30:07.000000000 | Looking forward to the formal comments:P  Dirty bit is a good idea but I debated in my mind about two uploading model many times:  - Multiple CPU calls to upload several dirty pieces. Don't waste any bandwidth but    start uploading process many times   - Single CPU calls to upload continuous range with non-necessary flush. Waste bandwidth but start uploading process only once.   Maybe a high-low dirty range is a balanced way?  >   SetNumWorkgroups(x, y, z);    SetBufferLength();  Using function name instead of enum is a great idea!  If we have new "RootSignature" object, I'd like to put this immediate data block struct/class in RootSignatureBase. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 164 | 2024-09-11 02:34:33.000000000 | >, but for tracking I believe dirty bits are better because they allow easy masking with the pipeline's mask of used constants.  You convinced me. I agree that the dirty bits is a necessary and simple state tracking struct. What's in my mind is: ``` // All known internal immediate data usages. Offset (and // size for some special  // internal immediate data usages like arrayLengthFromUniform) are initialized to // std::max<uint32_t> or some invalid values. uint32_t VertexIndexOffset = 0; uint32_t BufferLengthOffset = invalid;  // Values for each internal immediate data; std::array<uint32_t, 2> firstIndexFirstInstance = {}; std::vector<uint32_t> bufferLengths;  // Or a uniform vector. Vector could reserve size after initialization. With  // known offsets, it could store all values continuous. // std::vector<uint32_t> allValues.  // Bits to represent whether pipeline need the internal immediate data,  // initialize once and never changes again. Bits mUsedConstants;  // DirtyBits represent all known internal immediate data usages DirtyBits mAllInternalImmediateDataStates;  ``` |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/PipelineLayoutD3D12.cpp | 340 | 2024-09-11 02:34:33.000000000 | >// In PipelineBase?  In PipelineBase. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/PipelineLayoutD3D12.h | 55 | 2024-09-10 08:30:07.000000000 | >map<key, Ref<RootSignature>> with RootSignature being our new object that contains info about the D3D12 root signature.  That would be an option. Structure would be clear if we define a new "RootSignature" for all native backends. It lives between GPUPipelineLayout and GPUPipeline and stores real backend rootsignature. Pipeline holds it for querying some info. (And it could hold commandRootSignature). What's more, this "rootsignature" could be held in PipelineBase and BindGroupStateTrackers.  Considering the change size to address the complete design and the truth that we have limited internal immediate data, another option would be we keep current implementation for now (waste some immediate data slots but keeps the structure clean) :). |

| [208734](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/208734) | dawn | src/dawn/native/vulkan/ComputePipelineVk.h | 69 | 2024-10-01 01:26:27.000000000 | Yes. | [2180](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/2180) | dawn | examples/Animometer.cpp | 127 | 2018-11-05 13:50:20.000000000 | ComboDescriptor could have a private member that's    uint32_t cColorAttachments[kMaxColorAttachments]  that shadows the colorAttachments of the RenderPipeline and in the constructor we do colorAttachments = &cColorAttachments. (the c prefix is for combo).  This way user code doesn't have do declare an extra variable and instead can just do:   descriptor.cColorAttachments[0] = foo |

It is ok to create extra objects just to destroy them: they will disappear and the cost of creation / destruction will be small compared to the pipeline compilation. |
| [2180](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/2180) | dawn | examples/Animometer.cpp | 136 | 2018-11-05 13:50:20.000000000 | Overall this initialization would look like this:      utils::ComboRenderPipelineDescriptor descriptor; // Defaults are set already     descriptor.vertexModule = vsModule; // entrypoint defaults to "main"     descriptor.fragmentModule = fsModule; // entrypoint defaults to "main"     descriptor.numColorAttachments = 1;     descriptor.cColorAttachmentFormats = GetPreferredSwapChainTextureFormat();     // No cColorAttachments because the WebGPU IDL doesn't allow setting attachments with holes (i.e. attachments 0 and 2 but not 1). Might change in the future though.     descriptor.hasDepthStencilAttachment = true;     descriptor.depthStencilFormat = dawn::TextureFormat::D32FloatS8Uint;     // blendState is filled with defaults. Could even be the same blendstate object.      pipeline = device.CreateRenderPipeline(&descriptor);  I think similar simplifications could done everywhere. The point of the combo render pipeline is to require less typing ;) |

| [2180](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/2180) | dawn | src/tests/unittests/WireTests.cpp | 393 | 2018-12-10 13:17:24.000000000 | I'll step back and explain what happens in the wire tests. Basically what happens in that the regular dawn calls are like "client" calls in a client-server world, and get serialized in some buffer. Then when "FlushClient" is called, the "server" deserializes the commands and calls them. In this test instead of calling dawn_native we make it call mock_dawn so we can perform checks and fake the behavior of dawn_native.  This means that for almost all calls to dawnFoo we must have an EXPECT_CALL(api, Foo()) and if dawnFoo returns a value we need to create a fake object to return using myObject = api.GetNewMyObject and add WillOnce(Return(myObject)) to the EXPECT_CALL. If we don't do this, GMock will helpfully return nullptr for functions returning pointers.  The return of nullptr here is not what the wire server expects and makes it skip calls to dawnDeviceCreateRenderPipeline, causing the error you saw.  To fix this we need to add expectations and return "api" objects for all sub-objects. |

| [30541](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/30541) | dawn | src/dawn_native/BlitTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 68 | 2020-10-20 12:14:30.000000000 | We're working in 2D so if we need to support rotations, 2 by 2 is enough, if we need to support translations then 3 by 3 is enough.  But why not do the computations in the shader like for ANGLE's BlitGL shader?  https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/master:third_party/angle/src/libANGLE/renderer/gl/BlitGL.cpp;l=1286  Basically vertices could be generated by the shader, then the transform would be specified in the uniform buffer. All the data apart from the source texture would be in that single uniform buffer. It might be possible to have a single VS and a single FS that look at the uniform buffer and make all the decisions. Then you can have a single ShaderModule that contains both the vertex and the fragment. |

| [46422](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/46422) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2021-04-02 06:50:02.000000000 | LGTM with the fix of semantics, thanks Austin for catching this! |
| [46422](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/46422) | dawn | src/dawn_native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 57 | 2021-04-02 10:30:33.000000000 | This is a bit complicated when a Y-flip is just v_texcoord.y = 1.0 - v_texcoord.y. It would be simpler with a separate argument in the uniform buffer. Ok to simplify as a follow-up though. |
| [46422](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/46422) | dawn | src/tests/end2end/CopyTextureForBrowserTests.cpp | 177 | 2021-04-02 06:50:02.000000000 | Does this match what we want to do to implement things in the Blink side? My understanding is that in Blink some source textures will be Y flipped and some not, so Y-flip should control the flipping of the source, not of the destination rect.  For example if I do a copy of pixel (0, 0) of a 2x2 Y-flipped texture, I want it to copy pixel (0, 1) and not (0, 0) (then flip it on itself, so still 0, 0).  Of course both approaches are just as powerful but what's currently done necessitates more work on the Blink side to adjust the source rect instead of just passing arguments to Dawn.  Tests caught the issue, that's good! |

| [116924](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/116924) | dawn | src/dawn/native/CopyTextureForBrowserHelper.cpp | 101 | 2023-01-13 09:50:01.000000000 | The terminology here is weird: Textures are just 2D arrays of data and their origin is always texel (0, 0). The don't have bottom-left, top-left or anything, they don't exist in the physical world.  I think what we should say is:  > The NDC to framebuffer space transform maps inverts the Y coordinate such that NDC [-1, 1] (resp [-1, -1]) maps to framebuffer space [0, 0] (resp [0, height-1]). So we need to undo this flip when converting positions to texcoords.  We could also have the shader just say:    output.texcoords = texcoord[VertexIndex] * uniforms.scale + uniforms.offset;  and the comment put at [1] instead (and the condition flipped) so that we have less shader operations. But that's three vertex shader invocations so maybe it doesn't matter. |

| [118800](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/118800) | dawn | src/dawn/native/vulkan/external_memory/MemoryServiceManager.h | 30 | 2023-02-07 13:09:25.000000000 | IMHO the structure could be (but would also like Austin's opinion):   - ServiceManager which is renamed to Service that multiplexes the interface and dispatches on the ExternalImageType by itself, so that users of this code don't need to know that there are multiple service (the implementation details are hidden).  - Service renamed ServiceImplementation that just the interface.  - FDService, DMABufService etc.  ExternalImageHandle should probably be changed to not just be a typedef to something but a structure that contains the ExternalImageType and the various types of supported handles (or a uint64_t that's dynamically cast to the correct type? Not sure).  This means that the rest of the code basically doesn't have to change at all to support multiple different types of handles at runtime. (and doesn't have to care at all about OS-specifics)  Ditto for semaphore. |

| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/tint/lang/wgsl/ast/transform/multiplanar_external_texture_test.cc | 387 | 2024-04-15 14:57:47.000000000 | The clamping here seems incorrect because it clamps to half a texel inside of the whole texture instead of half a texel inside the visible rect.  What should happen here is:  ``` var modifiedCoords = params.samplingCoordTransform * vec3f(coord, 1); var plane0ClampedCoords = clamp(modifiedCoords, params.plane0VisibleRectMin, params.plane0VisibleRectMax);  if (params.numPlanes == 1) {   color = sample with plane0ClampedCoords } else {   var plane1ClampedCoords = clamp(modifiedCoords, params.plane1VisibleRectMin, params.plane1VisibleRectMax);   color = sample with plane0ClampedCoords and plane1ClampedCoords } ```  In ExternalTexture.cpp the bounds can be computed by doing:  ``` mat2x3 coordTransformMatrix // the one we had before this change. vec2 visibleRectBounds0 = Mul(coordTransformMatrix, vec2(0, 0)); vec2 visibleRectBounds1 = Mul(coordTransformMatrix, vec2(1, 1)); vec2 visibleRectBoundsMin = Min(visibleRectBounds0, visibleRectBounds1); vec2 visibleRectBoundsMax = Max(visibleRectBounds0, visibleRectBounds1);  vec2 plane0HalfTexel = Div(vec2(1, 1), vec2(plane1 subresource size)); vec2 plane0VisibleRectMin = Add(visibleRectBoundsMin, plane0HalfTexel); vec2 plane0VisibleRectMax = Sub(visibleRectBoundsMax, plane0HalfTexel);  // Ditto for plane1 ```  Can we have a basic test for a dual plane setup that would have found this issue, including a parametrization for flipY and rotation? |
| [181081](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/181081) | dawn | src/tint/lang/wgsl/ast/transform/multiplanar_external_texture_test.cc | 561 | 2024-04-16 08:12:49.000000000 | These are linear transforms. We want to get from [0, 1]x[0, 1] to [0, width]x[0, height]. That's just a scaling. `sampleTransform` work with normalized coordinates as input and output so we need to do:  ``` loadXform = toTexelSpace * sampleXform * toNormalized ```  Where both `toTexelSpace` and `toNormalized` are scaling matrices. `toTexelSpace` goes from `[0, 1]^2 to [0, width]x[0, height]` (so ScaleMatrix(plane0Width, plane0Height)) and `toNormalized` is going from `[0, apparentVisibleWidth]x[0, apparentVisibleHeight]` to `[0, 1]^2` so a Scale with the inverse of the apparent visible size.  Note that I used the word apparent, because this is visibleSize, but with the two coordinates swapped if the rotation is 90 or 270. |

I still don't understand why the loadMatrix cannot be computed easily from the sampleMatrix.  The bounds for loading would be the `visibleRectBoundsMin/Max` computed above (up to an off-by-one error) |

I have other ideas on how to get rid of it in the future. let transformedCoords = round(params.loadTransformationMatrix * vec3(coord, 1)); let plane0Coords = vec2<i32>(transformedCoords);   let plane1Coords = vec2<i32>(transformedCoords * factor); ```  Because we start with a clamp to the displayVisibleSize, we know that all transformed coordinates will be in bounds. |

| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2024-09-11 14:19:40.000000000 | Do you know when the HLSL translation adds uses of useVertexIndex / useInstanceIndex? My understanding is that we only need to add the variables when the FirstIndexOffset transforms does something useful so we should be good?  We pass information in random places in the D3D12 backend but for all of arglengths, firstIndexOffset and NumWorkgroup we pass binding points to Tint. Right now these have to be different bindings points because Tint will generate different ConstantBuffer<> for each, but Dawn can still compute them as a single bunch of root constants except that ah ... but then the root signature depends on them instead of only the total immediate size.  Ok so we have a chicken and egg problem where we need to move these transforms to use immediates when we don't have support for them yet in the D3D12 backend.  So let's do things in this order:   - Add some skeleton support of immediates in the D3D12 backend (still with full dirty bits, caching of root signature etc), and use it for arglength only.  - Move Tint to allow use of var<immediate> for argLength (as an option) and make the HLSL option take a register space + register number for the immediates, as well as an offset for where the arglength should be.  - Move Tint to allow use of immediate for numworkgroups, make the D3D12 backend use that.  - Move Tint to allow use of immediate for vertex/index, use it in D3D12 backend.  - Add support for immediates in WGSL, plump it in the frontend API, have it all work in D3D12.  - Remove options to use non-immediates for the various Tint transforms. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2024-09-10 12:22:09.000000000 | Thank you for your patience, this is a most interesting read as it shows the set of all the things a complete implementation needs to care about.  One structural thing to change is that the Render/ComputePipeline should explicitly know where each root constant used is located, and pass that explicitly to the HLSL compilation. Querying stuff after transforms is actually something that the Tint team wants to remove over time, it just hasn't done it for HLSL yet.  So RenderPipelineD3D12 would during initialization completely determine which root constants are needed and their offsets in the register space used for all root constants and pass that to TranslateHLSL. So I think a pre-CL for this would be to change the current Dawn to do that and stack the immediate CL on top (it allows landing that part without waiting for the rest of the design to stick).  So that's two pre-CLs that we can do:   - Making Compute/RenderPipelineD3D12 push to HLSL compilation the register offset / space of the builtins and which builtins are used.  - Landing immediateDataSize in PipelineLayout + limit, validation and validation tests.   I'm pretty convinced that we can have a small solution for handling root constants using dirty bits that would be faster and more ergonomic to use than a whole extra object. Though we'll have to be careful when to consider things dirty (for example a pipeline change should dirty all the bits used by the pipeline as a first approximation, but DispatchIndirect with NumWorkgroups set in the indirect commands shouldn't need to apply NumWorkgroups directly since we don't know what it is).  Let me know if you'd like a small meeting to discuss all of this, I know it's a lot but a clear solution is in sight! |

Though it seems that `usesInstanceIndex` is missing on EntryPointMetadata? |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 160 | 2024-09-10 12:22:09.000000000 | This would just set the values and the dirty bits, skipping setting the root constant here would be done when we try to apply the values before a draw / dispatch. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 164 | 2024-09-10 12:22:09.000000000 | Either a dirty bit or min-max works for the actual call to SetRootConstants, but for tracking I believe dirty bits are better because they allow easy masking with the pipeline's mask of used constants. The problem otherwise is that it becomes difficult to find that the min in the RootConstant in the stack variable is the actual min for the pipeline, or how to update it if the pipeline doesn't use that min variable.  So we can do min/max (using ScanForward / ScanBackward) or a few calls for `Somehow call setRootConstants correctly.`, but the tracking would still use dirty bits. WDYT?  > If we have new "RootSignature" object, I'd like to put this immediate data block struct/class in RootSignatureBase.  Deduplicating the code between backends would be nice, but I don't think that it is possible here because they all have their own special immediates to set for workaround / emulation. They also use different mechanisms to set them. D3D12 and Vulkan are similar but Metal would use setBytes, D3D11 a dynamic uniform buffer and GL just GL uniforms. So overall handling of immediate, both in the layout, the shader module and the command buffer would be per-backend. The frontend just says how many the user needs. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 164 | 2024-09-09 16:51:38.000000000 | I need to think about this a bit more, but I think we could try to have a simpler data layout for this instead of reaching out in the pipeline all the time.  Something like:  ``` // Defined in pipeline layout. ConstantIndex = ityp::TypedInteger<uint8_t, struct ConstnatIndexT>;  // Common constants ConstantIndex immediateStart = 0; ConstnatIndex bufferLengthStart = immediateStart + 4;  // Constants for compute ConstantIndex numWorkgroup = bufferLengthStart + N; ConstantIndex maxConstantComputes = numWorkgroup + 3;  struct RootConstants<compute vs. render> {    // Helpers to set dirty + values    SetNumWorkgroups(x, y, z);    SetBufferLength();        ityp::bitset<maxofcompute vs render> dirty;    std::array<maxofcompute vs render, uint32_t> values; };  RootConstants rootconstants;  At some point before draw:     // Aplpy missing constants     Get mask of used constants from the pipeline.     Or it with the dirty constants.     Somehow call setRootConstants correctly.      Of course dirtying also sometimes needs to happen when switching root signature or w/e. ```  Sry for messy comment, I have to leave for something but wanted to get this in today. |
| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 164 | 2024-09-11 14:19:40.000000000 | IMHO we could even do more explicitly and have structure containing all the data that we reinterpret_cast as a bunch of uint32_t for setting via root constants:  ``` struct CommonImmediates {     uint32_t immedates[kMaxImmediates];     uint32_t bufferLength[kMaxDynamicBuffers]; }  struct RenderImmediateValues : CommonImmediates {     uint32_t vertexIndexOffset;     uint32_t instanceIndexOffset;     // ... };  class CommonImmediates<Value> {   public:     const size_t kValueCount = sizeof(Value) / sizeof(uin32_t);      // Const getters for calling setRootConstant, or retreiving the data for other purpose     const Value* operator -> () const {return reinterpret_cast<const Value*>(&mData);}     const std::ityp_array<uint32_t, kValueCount>& AsUint32() const { ... }      // Setters     void SetImmediates(offset, count, uint32_t* values) {         for (ConstantIndex i : Range(count)) {             ConstantIndex index = offset + i;             mDirty \\|= index;             this->immediates[i] = values[index];         }     }          void OnPipelineChange(PipelineD3D12 pipeline) {         mDirty \\|= pipelineD3D12->GetConstantsMask();     }          void SetRootConstants(Id3D12CommandList*, Mak pipelineConstantMask, Mask requiredConstant = all) {         Mask constantsToApply = mDirty & requiredConstant & pipelineConstantMask;         // Apply them         mDirty \\|= ~constantsToApply;     }     protected:     // Only child class is allowed to mutate     operator -> () {return reinterpret_cast<Value*>(&mData);}          // This is necessary to be char to not have UB     alignas(Value) char [kValueCount * sizeof(uin32_t)] mDataata;     ityp::bitset<kValueCount, ConstantIndex> mDirty; };  class RenderImmediates : CommonImmediates< RenderImmediateValues > {    void SetVertexAndInstanceIndex(uint32_t vertex, uint32_t instance) {         mDirty \\|= ...;         this-> vertexIndexOffset = ...;         // same for instance     } }; ```  Then when we do other backends we can see how much of that can be reused if at all. |

| [205314](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/205314) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/PipelineLayoutD3D12.cpp | 340 | 2024-09-10 12:22:09.000000000 | Ultimately the pipeline should be the one computing how many root constants it needs and what each one corresponds to but the root signature only depends on how many root constants are needed. What do you imagine the code reuse would achieve? |

Doesn't it work to handle everything in the Vulkan backend by   - Adding some map in PipelineLayout from push constant bytes to VkPipelineLayout.  - Dirtying bindgroups if the immediate data range changed, similar to what we do in [d3d12](https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp;drc=f522344e45882da4c7f7cb1b3a0a7bd747d654bb;l=481)   It seems like it would be much less invasive than the suggestion in this CL. WDYT? |

So at init we'd do:  ```  mCachedCreateInfo.pushConstantRange.stageFlags =             VK_SHADER_STAGE_FRAGMENT_BIT \\| VK_SHADER_STAGE_COMPUTE_BIT \\| VK_SHADER_STAGE_VERTEX_BIT;         mCachedCreateInfo.pushConstantRange.offset = 0; ```  Then here:  ``` if (immediateDataSize == 0) {   mCachedCreateInfo.layout.pPushConstantRanges = nullptr; } else {   mCachedCreateInfo.pushConstantRange.size = internalImmediateDataSize;   mCachedCreateInfo.layout.pPushConstantRanges = &mCachedCreateInfo.pushConstantRange; } ```  Although now I have a concern about that cache for future multithreading. What about:  Returns the offset of the member in the packed immediates of the pipeline.  The pointer-to-member is a pointer into the structure containing all the potential immediates. However pipelines don't need all of them and use a compacted layout with immediates in the same order, just some of them skipped.  For example the pipeline mask 11001111, representing "userConstants: 4 \\| trivial_constants: 0 (2 at most)\\|clamp_frag:2", maps to pipeline immediate constant layout: "userConstants:4 \\| clamp_frag:2". |

| [235154](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/235154) | dawn | src/dawn/native/Limits.h | 39 | 2025-04-08 11:50:07.000000000 | I feel that this could be improved a lot: Dawn doesn't want to care about `Limits` and chained descriptors at all and all these methods should work on `CombinedLimits` for simplicity. What we need though it is a single function `MaybeError UnpackLimitsIn(const Limits* limits, CombinedLimits* out);` that's used to validate the chained structs are the allowed one, and copies data in a `CombinedLimits`. This way internally all the code is consistent and doesn't need to care about the chain. (and doesn't need to care about what's allowed vs. not because that validation will have already been done). |

| [250974](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/250974) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 1283 | 2025-07-09 09:58:50.000000000 | We need something new here because in D3D12 a root signature layout change invalidates the whole root signature. So as a first approximation, if the layout changes, we need to set all immediates as dirty. |
| [250974](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/250974) | dawn | src/dawn/native/d3d12/CommandBufferD3D12.cpp | 1283 | 2025-07-09 12:08:21.000000000 | I'm not sure that an example will help. It's more important to understand how D3D12 is specified as understanding the exact semantic leads to better solutions.  What I mean is that when the call at [2] happens, all the contents of the root signature, constants, descriptors and descriptor tables is invalidated. Which is why we pass `false` at [3] for `CanInheritBindGroups`. We should have something similar for root constants as they get invalidated the same way. |

| [219634](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/219634) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsLayout.h | 78 | 2025-01-08 02:22:36.000000000 | One reason we track this is that pipeline also needs to know the layout info so that it can record required internal constant during the constructor (not initialize) phase. We have to do this because the cache dawn used for pipeline (cache: https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/RenderPipeline.cpp;drc=44eb9f0dd0854e826da5e03b358c872cfdc5b0e9;l=1087) See this CL https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/220594/14 file src/dawn/native/RenderPipeline.cpp and file src/dawn/native/vulkan/RenderPipelineVk.cpp.  Pipeline needs a bit set so that in the final step, which ImmediateConstantTracker::UploadImmediateConstants(implemented in each native backend with different parameter list due to different graphics APIs) is called. we could have things like: ``` auto needsToUpload = dirtyBits & pipelineRequiredBits for (auto i : IterateBitSet(needsToUpload)) {   // move from immediateTracker to intermediate array so that we can call   // call a real uploading graphics API. } ``` The table helps to save we calculate layout in both place(I think?). |

| [219634](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/219634) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsLayout.h | 85 | 2025-01-03 05:43:33.000000000 | (Using this place for more details about the idea how to use these bits and table)  - In pipeline creation phase, pipeline needs to know the required constant bits, and that's that part in stacked CL. Works like this: Backend pipeline --Pass required internal constants type---> RenderPipeline/ComputePipeline --Get element from table and set constant block bits ---> PipelineBase --- Hash the pipeline constant mask---> finished.  - In command buffer phase, which constants needs to be set and update, we do things like: ``` // Using tracker to set immediate constants. tracker->OnSetPipeline(...) ... tracker->SetImmediateData(...); tracker->SetClampFragDepth(min, max); ... // Before draw or dispatch call, using tracker->uploadConstants() auto requiredUploadedBit = pipeline->requiredBits & mDirtyBits & excludeExternalBits for (auto i : IterateBitSet(requiredUploadedBit)) {    mUploadedVector.push_back(tracker->GetContent().Get<uint32_t>(i * elementSize)); } // Upload user external immediate data // Call backend push constant related graphics API to do real update. ``` |

For example, we might have "userConstants: 4 \\| trivial_constants: 0 (2 at most)\\|clamp_frag:2". In mask, it represents by 11001111, and to set clamp_frag depth offset correctly in shader, we need to calculate the real offset, which is `4 * kImmediateConstantElementByteSize` instead of `6 * kImmediateConstantElementByteSize`. |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsLayout.h | 89 | 2025-02-07 07:18:44.000000000 | Thanks for pointing out! Update the comment. |

| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsLayout.h | 95 | 2025-02-05 07:16:17.000000000 | Done |

| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsTracker.cpp | 41 | 2025-02-07 07:18:44.000000000 | Done |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsTracker.cpp | 41 | 2025-02-06 09:28:57.000000000 | Oh, you're right. This is trying to handle the case like https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/tests/end2end/FragDepthTests.cpp;drc=ddf744c3326de9291039d88f207981f205c7704a;l=77: ``` pass.setViewport(); pass.setPipeline(); // And this will invalidate the viewport we set previously and fails some dawn tests ``` So we invalidate all things when setPipeline will fail this case.  It seems we need to find a better time to invalidate all of them. |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsTracker.h | 45 | 2025-02-10 10:28:51.000000000 | Passing 'RenderPipeline' and 'ComputePipeline' here because we need to call `ToBackend(Pipeline)->GetVKLayout()`. So maybe we should store pipeline in each backend implementation instead of storing in base class? |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsTracker.h | 92 | 2025-02-11 10:33:21.000000000 | Got your point. Let's keep the TODO. |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsTracker.h | 92 | 2025-02-11 09:36:02.000000000 | Sry but which TODO? The compatible layout we talked as TODO in previous has been addressed, I think? Because we always update dirty bits to pipeline mask when setting pipeline. So it works like: pipeline0 1111011 --> dirty 1111011 ... pipeline1 1110011 -> dirty 1110011 In this mode, compatible has been achieved? |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/vulkan/CommandBufferVk.cpp | 199 | 2025-02-07 03:02:08.000000000 | OK, I think now I understand the whole idea. What you proposed is a thing like "uniform buffer lives in encoder instead of pipeline" and pipeline pick data from that encoder based on their immediate data range definition. |
| [221274](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/221274) | dawn | src/dawn/native/vulkan/CommandBufferVk.cpp | 199 | 2025-02-07 07:18:44.000000000 | >We should make sure that the push constants are all dirty when we start so we know to call vkCmdPushConstant.  I think we might not need to set all dirty bits considering `OnSetPipeline` will overwrite the dirty bits. All we need to do is to ensure content init values are all zero.  >Also double check that immediates are all defaulted to 0 at the start.  Mark constructors to `=default` to ensure the behaviour here https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/ImmediateConstantsTracker.h;drc=bd4da62dd5bf7f9138c030efeee44c375d536ffb;l=68 not been overwrite .  BTW, I invalidate all dirty bits at [b] to ensure no duplicate uploading. |

| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2025-07-14 08:47:26.000000000 | This CL add a new transform for array length, which pick array length from immediate blocks. Assume both array length transform (from uniform and from immediate) will live together for a while for different backend until we handles management mechanism in API side.  PTAL, thanks.  Note, not sure how to do fuzz tests update because we require a ImmediateConstantLayout object. Any suggestions? |

| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate.cc | 75 | 2025-07-16 08:37:43.000000000 | Thanks for the info. Let me add a config to control whether we use "pass values through function parameters". For D3D11 backend, immediate is indeed backed by a uniform buffer. But we could check on vulkan, d3d12 and metal backend. |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate.cc | 75 | 2025-07-16 08:48:40.000000000 | >in response to a metal shader compiler bug where in certain cases the compiler would take a long time  Oh, I just missed the info about it is a metal shader compiler bug. Metal "immediate" is quite similar to "internal uniform buffer" instead of specific concepts in vulkan (push constants) and d3d12 (root constants).  And in API side, array_length is already uploaded by `SetBytes`(`SetVertexBytes\\|SetFragmentBytes`) https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/dawn/src/dawn/native/metal/CommandBufferMTL.mm;drc=26482a8fc4b21563e377885df71b24b439f25427;l=507.  So I think `immediate` doesn't helps the issue :( |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate.cc | 295 | 2025-07-16 09:00:52.000000000 | Done |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate.cc | 297 | 2025-07-15 13:49:23.000000000 | immediate buffers have same alignment requirements as uniform buffer. So we have to keep this. |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate.cc | 303 | 2025-07-15 13:49:23.000000000 | uniform uses if condition(condition to check whether uniform buffer has been issued) to prevent re-calculate this again and again. However, immediate block is an external object, and it needs the num_elements in immediate transform and we can reuse this info here to emit calculations. |

| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate_test.cc | 100 | 2025-07-21 02:10:19.000000000 | Is it possible to detect whether `Robustness` requires `arrayLength` through tint resolve phase? But for now, I'd like to keep Dawn API side as the one who decide what's the immediate block looks like (might generate unused immediate blocks as the previous test cases) |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate_test.cc | 100 | 2025-07-17 00:56:15.000000000 | Plan to fix it by making `buffer_size_start_offset` and `buffer_sizes_array_elements_num ` to `std::optional` first and make `immediate_data_config` empty(Assume in API side we detect no array_length usage and not pass buffer sizes at all) in these tests to remove these non used immediate block. |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate_test.cc | 100 | 2025-07-22 01:21:13.000000000 | >Do we expect a performance hit from having an unnecessary immediate block?   It depends on backends. Metal should be fine but D3D12 might be affected. We need to allocate space in root signature and a large root signature might cause spill in some hardware platform.  I think it is Ok to keep the unnecessary immediate block and look back when we indeed hit a performance issue related to it. WDYT? |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate_test.cc | 100 | 2025-07-16 00:47:19.000000000 | So the logic is that API side shouldn't provide `buffer_sizes_offset` if it knows no array_lenghts are needed.We want to detect whether we need to upload array length in API side so that we can allocate immediate space for these internal constants.  Would it be possible that the API side uses tint reflection info and possible running transforms info (By detecting requests?) to decide whether we need these array length constants? |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate_test.cc | 100 | 2025-07-15 13:49:23.000000000 | Unfortunately, yes. The logic we do with immediate block is that we provide the attributes first and issue them. Then we go into array_length trasnform. So we miss the chance to not issue immediate block in some cases. Do you have some ideas? |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_immediate_test.cc | 100 | 2025-07-16 02:58:26.000000000 | >or it could just check for array length calls and dawn decides if the buffer is needed.  Exactly, maybe a `array_Length_used` attribute in metadata. Slightly concern about transforms in backend added array_length usage. But Dawn side could know the exact transforms it runs so maybe not a problem. |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_uniform.cc | 435 | 2025-07-15 01:55:12.000000000 | We're considering a mechanism that fallback some root signature things into uniform if root signature is too large or OOB (https://issues.chromium.org/issues/428802462). So maybe we should keep the array length from uniforms. |
| [252614](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/252614) | dawn | src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/array_length_from_uniform.cc | 435 | 2025-07-15 13:49:23.000000000 | (If we add spill logic) >buffer? Or does it spill all of them to uniform? We (plan to) spill all of them to uniform.  >If we sometimes have to spill, is the benefit here that immediates are typically faster? (If you mean spill happens in driver) I think it should be the same perf as uniform. But if no spill happens in driver, immediate should be faster.  However, I made my mind to replace uniform with immediate until we find some real case that keeping them in root signature cause problem. |

| [30541](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/30541) | dawn | src/dawn_native/InternalPipelineStore.cpp | 28 | 2020-10-21 02:15:45.000000000 | I understand the concerns about have more stuff precompiled in the future. That's definitely an important improvement we should make later. I think though we should try to use more of the Dawn API to keep it simple for now.  We can have a simple InternalStore mapping as Corentin said from Key => ShaderModule and/or Key => Pipeline.  To support better precompilation in the future, we can add a way for Dawn to Serialize its InternalStore to a binary blob. Linking Dawn into Chrome then becomes a two-step process where you build Dawn, use Dawn to generate the serialized internal pipelines, and then embed the serialized blob into the binary. At runtime, we load Dawn and pass in a pointer to the serialized internal pipelines. This is good because it:  - reuses existing code paths to create the internal pipelines  - keeps the precompilation optimization mostly outside of Dawn  - lets embedders of Dawn (like Skia) also precompile pipelines  - fits in nicely with the pipeline cache which we also load from a serialized blob. the internal pipelines would essentially have their own VkPipelineCache or D3D12 pipeline library |
| [30541](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/30541) | dawn | src/dawn_native/InternalPipelineStore.cpp | 28 | 2020-10-21 04:36:24.000000000 | Correcting myself on this: one day if we optimize this to precompile - it still can't compile pipelines using the graphics driver since that would be driver-dependent - so no vkPipelineCache and D3D12 pipeline library. We could still compile shaders to SPIR-V/DXIL/MSL though. |
| [30541](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/30541) | dawn | src/dawn_native/InternalPipelineStore.cpp | 28 | 2020-10-21 02:31:02.000000000 | yea - basically always do it lazy, and when we want to optimize this, we preload the internal store the the precompiled pipelines / shader modules so that the compile never happens at runtime. |

| [36140](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/36140) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2020-12-22 18:48:59.000000000 | the hashing is certainly less optimal than looking up in a simple map. This was just a side thought. I think we should just do the map for now since we need to store all of the pipelines in some data structure anyway.  If the parameterization becomes more complex and we have a complex multikey of [dst format, num channels, ... , etc.] => pipeline mapping, then we should just rely on the frontend caching and store a simple std::vector<Ref<RenderPipelineBase>> here.  tbh I don't think there's a significant problem with having multiple pipelines. A typical app would probably have more pipelines than we have here. |
| [36140](https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/36140) | dawn | /PATCHSET_LEVEL |  | 2020-12-21 21:28:07.000000000 | Can we just have a map of texture format to Ref<RenderPipelineBase> to simplify the implementation? I'm not sure if it's so significant of a problem to have lots of cached objects. If the shaders are identical, the driver implementation should be able to deduplicate the state differences in pipelines when we add the backend pipeline caches. ^ If you want, we could measure this if we add a vkPipelineCache, and then check the serialized size after storing one pipeline, and then check again after storing many of different dst format.  If it's a problem, in the future, I think we could always implement some heuristics to prune the cache if it grows too large (like if an internal pipeline hasn't been used recently).  As a side note: I'm also curious what the cost of Dawn's frontend pipeline deduplication is. We already have a way to hash the descriptor and return a ref to an existing pipeline if there already is one. It would simplify things (slightly) to rely on that mechanism instead of having our own cache inside InternalPipelineStore. The main benefit though would be that if we added last-recently-used heuristics, we just need a simple LRU cache of Ref<RenderPipelineBase> and not care about the mapping of dstFormat->RenderPipelineBase |
